CEILING PUBLISHED WITH THE STATISTIC: 9-58% of top-decile rows have their own partner in the same decile (both sides of one clock), which is 50% by construction -- so the null is real and the measurement cannot exceed it by ranking magnitude alone. CONCLUSION: the current arms carry no tradable direction signal even at their most confident; what transfers is which MOMENTS are big, not which SIDE to take. ## 2026-08-13 — P-M2a: the sheet builder (implementation lane, D-002/D-005)
Built `engine/port_m2/` — the M2 sheet builder that turns any candidate id into the 14-section compact-text
decision view of PORT_M2_SHEETS_SPEC.md §1 (spec sha16 f14b5c773ccec5ba verified at every build).

What is new that did not exist before: the port never persisted a PER-EVENT MBP-1 stream (M0/M1 stop at
1-second grids plus a per-trade list), and S6 needs one, so `tape.py` extracts it from the raw payload files
with the m0 seal guard, decode loop and dominance convention reused verbatim, caching under
artifacts/cache/port/m2/events/. Measured 3.25M records/s on the Python decoder and an early-stop on the
time-ordered stream, so a session slice costs ~10s even on the 2.4GB yearly files.

Causality is a call, not a convention: every session-clock read goes through `CausalGuard.sec` /
`.at_decision` and every wall-clock read through `AvailSeries.latest(guard)`, so the leak fixture has exactly
one thing to attack. That design found a real leak vector in a COMMITTED receipt: `levels_v4`'s
`last_test_outcome` is resolved inside a FORWARD 15-minute window, so it is not knowable at the touch second
— the sheet shows PENDING until the resolution second passes, and the mutant that reads the stored column is
caught by 219 causal touches vs 1,202 end-of-session (test t09).

The honest tension: the spec asks for EVERY event of the final 90 seconds AND ~3-5k tokens per sheet. Measured
density is 91-4,421 events per 90s window, i.e. ~6x the whole token budget on a median candidate and ~25x on
the worst. The spec names its own reconciliation (episode-digest compression), so the raw window is
BUDGET-FILLED: digests always cover [T-690s, T-90s), the newest events stay raw until the S6 budget is spent,
and the older remainder of the 90s folds into the same gap-clustered digests — nothing dropped, no
minimum-size filter, `raw_cover_sec` reported per sheet (median 9.3s). The S6 budget is a policy knob and the
number is now measured, so the orchestrator can price it: ~25 tokens per extra second.

Results: 30/30 pilot sheets certified, 0 leak refusals, two-run byte identity 30/30 sheets + 30/30 appendices
+ 30/30 on-disk files with 0 diffs; leak fixture 6/6 red-first (both spec-named poisons plus four more);
15/15 tests with 15 mutants killed; new D-057 lag table with 21 series and business-day calendars read from
the data rather than a hand-maintained holiday list. Ten defects returned in evidence/port_m2/P_M2A_REPORT.md.

Taking a seat BLOCKS THE REST OF THE PHASE, so a flat threshold is the wrong object: the bar must be high while many arrivals remain and fall as the cell empties. With m arrivals still expected, a candidate is worth taking when it is plausibly the best of the m+1 acts still available — i.e. when it clears the (1 - c/(m+1)) quantile of the TRAINING BLOCK's score distribution, with m estimated causally as the training block's mean cell size minus the arrivals already seen. IT HAS NO FITTED KNOB: c is one pre-registered constant {0.5, 1, 2} and the rest is arithmetic, which matters a great deal under the search-adjusted-null law because a family that cannot be tuned cannot manufacture a winner. Added alongside CELLSOFAR (the within-cell running quantile), which exists for a different reason worth recording: the score was TRAINED on within-cell rank, so asking it to be comparable across a whole asset-day (DAYSOFAR) demands more than it was ever built to supply, while the running-max SECRETARY can only fire on a new record; CELLSOFAR asks exactly the question the score can answer — is this in the top (1-q) of THIS CELL so far. The causal policy family is now 24 wide: FIRST, 7 x TAU, 3 x DAYSOFAR, 4 x CELLSOFAR, 3 x OCCUPANCY, 3 x SECRETARY, 3 x SECRETARY_REENTRY. A METHOD POINT THAT COST A PATCH AND IS WORTH THE LINE: the search-adjusted luck bar is only a luck bar if it is computed over THE SAME FAMILY WIDTH the winner was selected from, so CAUSAL_BASELINE now runs the shuffled arm INSIDE its own sweep, cell for cell, arrival times preserved and only the row->score pairing destroyed. THE LESSON, and it belongs beside the exact-PID law already in run.sh: run.sh's whole contract is that liveness and death are addressed BY RECORDED PID and never by process-name matching — I used the registry correctly for the lane I meant to kill and then reached for a name pattern anyway 'to be sure', which is exactly the failure the law exists to prevent. NO pkill -f AGAINST A REGISTERED LANE'S COMMAND LINE, EVER; kill the recorded pid and its children and verify with `run.sh --list`. Two lanes that share a command line are indistinguishable to any name matcher by construction. Nothing was lost but ~2 minutes and no artifact was corrupted, because the race was broken before either process reached its write.

Every future arrival-score table in this program should carry tail-dollar columns and may carry AUC only as context.

THE METHODOLOGICAL FINDING (large): A_EV has COIN-FLIP AUC (0.48-0.51) yet +$766 tails; A_PBAR had 0.89 AUC and NEGATIVE tails — AUC is the WRONG diagnostic for arrival scores (tail dollars at the operating point is right), which also un-licenses the campaign's earlier 'no global signal at AUC 0.497' conclusion. Self-corrections recorded: a seed-0 overquote caught (E5 marked unquotable, within 1σ of zero), seed-averaging tested and downgraded (a wash), pkill self-kill relearned (exact-PID law). CAUTION ON ITS FACE: A_EV is so far a SELECTION STATISTIC, not a replayed policy — port-ev-policy2 (the 24-policy family with in-sweep luck bars) is running and ITS number is the only one that decides. Honest replayed causal remains $147.52 vs the $2,021+ causal bar.
2026-08-21 ~23:36Z FAIR-ENGINE ROUND LAUNCHED, AND FOR THE FIRST TIME THE ENGINE QUESTION IS WORTH ASKING (port-arrival-engines, 60 fits: LightGBM x2 configs, LightGBM-dart, CatBoost-ordered, vs the xgb incumbent, 5 seeds, E5/E6/E7). Every previous engine comparison this program ran — CatBoost YetiRank, LightGBM lambdarank, the whole final-pass block — was measured against the WITHIN-CELL RANKING objective the leak audit has now voided, and a pairwise ranker is invariant to any monotone transform inside a group; the engines were being compared on a quantity the deployment cannot consume, which is a large part of why they all read as noise around the champion. An ABSOLUTE REGRESSION ONTO DOLLARS is a different question and one engines genuinely differ at. THE MOTIVATION IS SPECIFIC, not a fishing trip: A_EV's measured weakness is its SEED VARIANCE (sd 0.35-2.7x its own mean), and variance is exactly where boosting implementations diverge — ordered boosting, dart's dropout, and leaf-wise vs depth-wise growth are all variance-side choices. A more stable expectancy fit at the same tail dollars is a real improvement; a less stable one at higher tail dollars is not, so sd_over_mean is on the face of the table beside the dollars. BINDING ON THIS TABLE AND ON EVERY ARRIVAL TABLE AFTER IT: the diagnostic is TAIL DOLLARS AT THE OPERATING POINT, never AUC. Tonight produced the clean demonstration — A_EV sign-AUC 0.478-0.507 with a +$766/trade tail, A_PBAR AUC 0.887-0.904 with a -$115 tail, same data, same night. AUC is carried as context only and nothing is ever selected on it. Grids are small and pre-registered in the module; the table promotes NOTHING on its own, since any winner must still clear the search-adjusted null of the causal policy family downstream.
